```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0001

stephanus_span: 58a-58c

  text_sha256: 5150661ca434905f8ff31d2fda55f256d191603e63c6cbf822ae07879a008f6c
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: report
content: Phaedo reports that according to the Athenians, during the annual sacred embassy to Delos the city must remain ritually pure and no one may be publicly executed until the ship returns from Delos.
greek_terms:
  - θεωρίαν
  - καθαρεύειν
  - δημοσίᾳ μηδένα ἀποκτεινύναι
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 58a-58c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 58a-58c
      start_marker: 58a
      end_marker: 58c
      start_char: 599
      end_char: 2208
      text_sha256: 5150661ca434905f8ff31d2fda55f256d191603e63c6cbf822ae07879a008f6c
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0267
limits: This record does not establish that the law was actually in force at this historical moment or that Socrates' death was caused by the law; it records only what Phaedo recounts as Athenian practice.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0002
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 60b-60c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 60b-60c
  start_marker: 60b
  end_marker: 60c
  start_char: 6315
  end_char: 7323
  text_sha256: c775c8b065557d50e523c30071a5de4bcfb3e7a8394566dadd8eb6baf8124cf8
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Pleasure and pain are opposites that do not easily come together but, if someone pursues one and obtains it, he is almost always compelled to take the other too, as if the two are attached from a single head.
greek_terms:
  - ἡδύ
  - λυπηρόν
  - ἐναντίον
  - κορυφῆς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 60b-60c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 60b-60c
      start_marker: 60b

      end_char: 7323
      text_sha256: c775c8b065557d50e523c30071a5de4bcfb3e7a8394566dadd8eb6baf8124cf8
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0268
limits: This record does not establish that the thesis is endorsed by anyone other than Socrates as reported by Phaedo; it is not framed as a general dialectical conclusion but as an observation occasioned by the bodily sensation after the shackle is removed.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0003
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 60c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 60c
  start_marker: 60c
  end_marker: 60c
  start_char: 6773
  end_char: 7323
  text_sha256: 17001f4135a1271dd358f41bfacc589aee77210543eed2ecb5b20b4ff7b23ead
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If Aesop had thought of it he would have composed a myth that a god, wanting to reconcile pleasure and pain when they were at war but unable to do so, fastened their heads together, and for that reason wherever one arrives the other follows afterward.
greek_terms:
  - Αἴσωπος
  - μῦθον
  - θεὸς
  - διαλλάξαι
  - πολεμοῦντα
  - συνῆψεν
  - κορυφάς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 60c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

      start_marker: 60c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0269
limits: The record reports what Socrates imagines Aesop would have composed under the earlier thesis about pleasure and pain; it is a speculative counterfactual, not a myth that Socrates claims Aesop actually wrote, and it is not examined dialectically.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0004
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 60e-61a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 60e-61a
  start_marker: 60e
  end_marker: 61a
  start_char: 7864
  end_char: 8797
  text_sha256: 0b2bd765e324eb6ae5af8627f48a26ca7f10f8c9993e7cbc7e2bc289dddca258
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Philosophy is the greatest music, and what Socrates used to do — namely, practicing philosophy — was what the recurring dream commanded when it said "make music and work at it."
greek_terms:
  - φιλοσοφίας
  - μεγίστης μουσικῆς
  - μουσικὴν ποίει καὶ ἐργάζου
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 60e-61a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 60e-61a
      start_marker: 60e
      end_marker: 61a
      start_char: 7864
      end_char: 8797
      text_sha256: 0b2bd765e324eb6ae5af8627f48a26ca7f10f8c9993e7cbc7e2bc289dddca258
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0270
limits: This is Socrates's retrospective construal of the dream command before the trial; the record does not establish that the dream command literally meant philosophy — Socrates's subsequent action (composing verses) and his caveat at 61a-b indicate that he later took the command more literally as well. The thesis is not dialectically examined in the present segment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0005
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 61a-61b

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: It is safer not to depart before discharging a religious obligation by composing poetry in obedience to the dream, if indeed the dream repeatedly commands the composition of popular music.
greek_terms:
  - ἀσφαλέστερον
  - ἀφοσιώσασθαι
  - ποιήσαντα ποιήματα
  - πιθόμενον τῷ ἐνυπνίῳ

final_status: left_standing

limits: This is Socrates's stated prudential-religious principle for his own conduct during the prison delay, not a universal method rule offered for dialectical examination; it is not tested in the present segment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0006

```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0009
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 62a

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 62a

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It is surprising if the rule against suicide is exceptionless, since for some people at some times it is better to be dead than alive, yet it is not holy for those people to benefit themselves — they must await another benefactor.
greek_terms:
  - ἁπλοῦν
  - ὅσιον
  - εὐεργέτην

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0011
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 62b-62c

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The gods are our caretakers and we humans are among the possessions (ktēmata) of the gods.
greek_terms:
  - θεοὺς εἶναι ἡμῶν τοὺς ἐπιμελουμένους
  - ἓν τῶν κτημάτων τοῖς θεοῖς

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0012

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0013
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 62c-62e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 62c-62e
  start_marker: 62c
  end_marker: 62e
  start_char: 11986
  end_char: 13371
  text_sha256: 4823e5fdf5f42d2397a2ec6e137673867e379578d2bc555dc2346574d04be86c
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If the gods are our caretakers and we are their possessions, then it is absurd that the wise should willingly leave this service — rather, the wise should be distressed at dying and the foolish should rejoice.
greek_terms:
  - τοὺς μὲν γὰρ φρονίμους ἀγανακτεῖν ἀποθνῄσκοντας πρέπει, τοὺς δὲ ἄφρονας χαίρειν
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 62c-62e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 62c-62e
      start_marker: 62c
      end_marker: 62e
      start_char: 11986
      end_char: 13371
      text_sha256: 4823e5fdf5f42d2397a2ec6e137673867e379578d2bc555dc2346574d04be86c
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0279
limits: Cebes frames this as a direct challenge to Socrates' earlier claim that philosophers are willing to die; he presents it as the likely (eikos) conclusion from the shared premises, setting up the response that follows beyond this segment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0014

stephanus_span: 63b-63c

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates asserts he would be wrong not to accept death if he did not expect to go to wise and good gods and to better dead men.
greek_terms:
  - παρὰ θεοὺς ἄλλους σοφούς τε καὶ ἀγαθούς
  - παρ’ ἀνθρώπους τετελευτηκότας ἀμείνους τῶν ἐνθάδε
  - ἠδίκουν ἂν
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0017

stephanus_span: 64c

  - τὴν τῆς ψυχῆς ἀπὸ τοῦ σώματος ἀπαλλαγήν
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0018

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The true philosopher does not take seriously the so-called pleasures of food, drink, or sex, nor the adornments of the body, but instead turns away from the body toward the soul as much as possible.
greek_terms:
  - ἥκιστα
  - οὐδαμῶς
  - ἀτιμάζειν
  - ἡ τοῦ τοιούτου πραγματεία οὐ περὶ τὸ σῶμα εἶναι

final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0283

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The philosopher, more than other men, separates the soul as much as possible from the body's communion.
greek_terms:
```

```yaml

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0021

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0022
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 65c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The soul reckons best when it is disturbed by none of the senses or pleasures and pains, becoming as far as possible alone by itself and reaching out for what is.
greek_terms:
  - λογίζεται
  - κάλλιστα
  - ὄντος
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 65c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0023
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 65d-65e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 65d-65e

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: There is a just itself, a beautiful itself, and a good itself, and none of these has ever been seen with the eyes or grasped by any bodily sense.
greek_terms:
  - δίκαιον αὐτὸ
  - καλόν
  - ἀγαθόν
  - οὐσίας
stance_events:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0024
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 65e-66a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 65e-66a
  start_marker: 65e
  end_marker: 66a
  start_char: 20386
  end_char: 21341
  text_sha256: 00beb5dc7f6d4f487eb6c988120d27dd39b4eefa95ce60196a8bba57e74c3b24
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Whoever approaches each thing most purely by thought alone, without relying on sight or dragging in any other sense, using pure thought by itself, will come closest to knowing each thing.
greek_terms:
  - αὐτῇ τῇ διανοίᾳ
  - εἰλικρινεῖ τῇ διανοίᾳ
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 65e-66a
    source_ref:

      end_marker: 66a

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0025
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 66b-66d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 66b-66d
  start_marker: 66b
  end_marker: 66d
  start_char: 21341
  end_char: 22683
  text_sha256: 1e0c6431cbcfaa0cd4ceb13b4b27da3e44843e3471a9121e00c80ffe247507a7
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "So long as we have the body and the soul is mixed together with such an evil, we will never adequately attain what we desire, namely the truth, because the body causes countless distractions: necessary nourishment, diseases, desires, fears, and fantasies."
greek_terms:
  - σῶμα
  - συμπεφυρμένη
  - κακοῦ
  - ἀληθές
  - ἀσχολίας
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 66b-66d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 66b-66d
      start_marker: 66b
      end_marker: 66d
      start_char: 21341
      end_char: 22683
      text_sha256: 1e0c6431cbcfaa0cd4ceb13b4b27da3e44843e3471a9121e00c80ffe247507a7
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0289
limits: The claim is presented as the view that genuine philosophers must hold; Socrates attributes this conclusion to all the previous points. It does not prove that the body is inherently evil, only that it obstructs the pursuit of truth. Does not establish that truth is attainable post-mortem.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0026
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 66c-66d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 66c-66d
  start_marker: 66c
  end_marker: 66d

  end_char: 22683
  text_sha256: 04b39c5e6c48b16c94df9343e85250796ecbc9d606ed77e7786d0267b9bbdbf4
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: All wars are caused by the acquisition of possessions, and we are compelled to acquire possessions because of the body, being enslaved to its care.
greek_terms:
  - πολέμους
  - χρημάτων
  - κτῆσιν
  - σῶμα
  - δουλεύοντες
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: The claim ties war to bodily desire for possessions but does not consider other possible causes of war. It is asserted as part of the argument that the body obstructs philosophy, not as an independent political thesis.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0027
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 66e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 66e
  start_marker: 66e

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If we are ever to know anything purely, we must be released from the body and behold the things themselves with the soul itself.
greek_terms:
  - καθαρῶς
  - ἀπαλλακτέον
  - αὐτῇ τῇ ψυχῇ
  - αὐτὰ τὰ πράγματα
stance_events:

    stephanus_span: 66e

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0291
limits: States a necessary condition for pure knowledge; does not prove that release from the body is sufficient for knowledge. Does not establish that the soul survives death.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0028
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 66e-67a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 66e-67a
  start_marker: 66e
  end_marker: 67a
  start_char: 22683
  end_char: 23473
  text_sha256: 764f9cacb16b3795c02a85d98d8aa8bc48690d4cdb74b623504dd61aad86434f
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If it is not possible to know anything purely with the body, then knowledge is either nowhere attainable or attainable only after death, when the soul is itself by itself apart from the body.
greek_terms:
  - καθαρῶς γνῶναι
  - τελευτήσασιν
  - χωρὶς τοῦ σώματος
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 66e-67a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 66e-67a
      start_marker: 66e
      end_marker: 67a

      end_char: 23473

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0292
limits: Presents a disjunction as the consequence of the earlier claim about bodily obstruction. It does not resolve which disjunct is true; that depends on whether the soul can know purely without the body. Does not independently prove post-mortem knowledge.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0029
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 67a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: While we live, we will be closest to knowing if we have as little association and communion with the body as possible, and keep ourselves pure from it, until the god himself releases us.
greek_terms:
  - ἐγγυτάτω
  - μηδὲν ὁμιλῶμεν
  - καθαρεύωμεν
  - ὁ θεὸς
stance_events:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0293
limits: "States a practical rule for embodied life: minimize bodily communion. Does not claim that this yields full knowledge, only that it brings one closest. The reference to divine release is not argued for here."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0031

stephanus_span: 67c

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0033

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Those who philosophize correctly practice dying, and death is least fearful to them among all human beings.

  - ἀποθνῄσκειν
  - μελετῶσι

    stephanus_span: 67e

final_status: left_standing

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0035
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 67e-68a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 67e-68a
  start_marker: 67e
  end_marker: 68a

  end_char: 25897
  text_sha256: b758a45067f1a08b64135068af5a1c539372967387ef7956ada5b8ac77d4293c
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It would be irrational for those who have been at variance with the body their whole life and desire to have the soul alone by itself to be distressed at death's arrival rather than go gladly where they may attain what they have loved — namely wisdom — and be rid of what they were at variance with.
greek_terms:
  - ἀλογία
  - φρονήσεως
  - διαβέβληνται
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 67e-68a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 67e-68a
      start_marker: 67e
      end_marker: 68a
      start_char: 24950
      end_char: 25897
      text_sha256: b758a45067f1a08b64135068af5a1c539372967387ef7956ada5b8ac77d4293c
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0299
limits: Presented as a conditional argument. Does not establish that philosophers actually do go gladly, only that grief would be inconsistent with their commitments.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0036

stephanus_span: 68b-68c

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The true philosopher will be convinced that he will encounter wisdom (phronēsis) purely nowhere else but in Hades.
greek_terms:
  - φρονήσει
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 68b-68c
    source_ref:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0037
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 68b-68c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 68b-68c
  start_marker: 68b
  end_marker: 68c
  start_char: 25897
  end_char: 26946
  text_sha256: c19c7cdb3777df337da84a02f0200d54d164b6c7261b86d124a9808bc2945c9c
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: A man who is distressed at the prospect of dying is not a philosopher but a lover of the body (philosōmatos), and the same man is also a lover of money (philochrēmatos) and a lover of honor (philotimos), either one or both.
greek_terms:
  - φιλοσώματος
  - φιλοχρήματος
  - φιλότιμος
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 68b-68c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 68b-68c

      end_char: 26946
      text_sha256: c19c7cdb3777df337da84a02f0200d54d164b6c7261b86d124a9808bc2945c9c
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0301
limits: This record does not establish that every lover of the body is necessarily a lover of money or honor; the claim allows either one or both. It also does not establish that the philosopher is entirely free of body-love, only that visible distress at death excludes the philosophical character.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0038

stephanus_span: 68b-68c

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: So-called courage (andreia) belongs most of all to those who are disposed in the philosophical way.
greek_terms:
  - ἀνδρεία
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 68b-68c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0039
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 68b-68c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 68b-68c
  start_marker: 68b
  end_marker: 68c

  end_char: 26946
  text_sha256: c19c7cdb3777df337da84a02f0200d54d164b6c7261b86d124a9808bc2945c9c
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Sōphrosynē (temperance), which the many call sōphrosynē — consisting in not being excited by desires but holding them in contempt and orderly control — belongs only to those who most of all hold the body in contempt and live in philosophy.
greek_terms:
  - σωφροσύνη
  - ἐπιθυμίας
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 68b-68c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 68b-68c
      start_marker: 68b
      end_marker: 68c

      end_char: 26946

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0303
limits: This record does not establish that the many's definition of sōphrosynē is correct; Socrates marks it as the popular conception. It also does not establish that only philosophers can be temperate — the claim is posed as a question for examination and Simmias's agreement is noted but not independently argued.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0040

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It would be great irrationality (alogia) for the true philosopher to fear death.
greek_terms:
  - ἀλογία
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 68b-68c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0304

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0041

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: Distress at the prospect of death is a sufficient sign (tekmērion) that a man is not a philosopher but a lover of the body.

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0042
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 69a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

  text_sha256: 7a15ad2bbcdbc2161f209862cf5a209b8e261d8f22fa87ff915100fa73f493e2
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The right exchange for virtue is not trading pleasures for pleasures, pains for pains, and fear for fear like coins of greater and lesser value, but rather there is only one right coin—wisdom—against which all these must be exchanged.
greek_terms:
  - φρόνησις
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 69a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 69a
      start_marker: 69a
      end_marker: 69a

      end_char: 28318
      text_sha256: 7a15ad2bbcdbc2161f209862cf5a209b8e261d8f22fa87ff915100fa73f493e2
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0306
limits: The record does not establish that Socrates endorses this thesis in his own voice; it is reported through Phaedo's narration of Socrates' speech. The claim does not address whether wisdom is itself a virtue or merely the medium of exchange for virtues.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0043

claim_kind: thesis
content: True virtue—courage, temperance, justice, and in sum all true virtue—exists only with wisdom, regardless of whether pleasures, fears, and all such things are added or taken away.

  - μετὰ φρονήσεως
  - ἀνδρεία
  - σωφροσύνη
  - δικαιοσύνη

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0044

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0046
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 69c

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: report
content: "The founders of the mystery rites were not contemptible but in truth long ago spoke in riddles: that whoever arrives in Hades uninitiated and unperfected will lie in mire, while the purified and perfected will dwell with the gods."

  - τελετὰς
  - ἀμύητος
  - ἀτέλεστος
  - βορβόρῳ
  - κεκαθαρμένος

review_status: accepted
```




```yaml

  text_sha256: 7055f83cce036c2aa56311dd23dfa51496e2ec692f639f7e3ba00447538bebff
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If generation were a straight line from one opposite only to the other without bending back or revolving in a circle, all things would end up with the same form, the same affection, and would cease to come into being.
greek_terms:
  - εὐθεῖά τις εἴη ἡ γένεσις
  - κύκλῳ περιιόντα
  - καμπὴν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 72b-72c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 72b-72c
      start_marker: 72b
      end_marker: 72c
      start_char: 35645
      end_char: 36632
      text_sha256: 7055f83cce036c2aa56311dd23dfa51496e2ec692f639f7e3ba00447538bebff
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0329
limits: This record registers the hypothetical thesis that Socrates introduces to frame the argument for cyclical generation. It does not assert that generation actually proceeds in a straight line; Socrates presents it as a counterfactual whose absurd consequences he will illustrate.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0070
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 72b-72c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If falling asleep existed but waking up did not reciprocally come to be out of being asleep, everything would ultimately end up asleep like Endymion and be nowhere visible.
greek_terms:
  - καταδαρθάνειν
  - ἀνεγείρεσθαι
  - ἀνταποδιδοίη
  - Ἐνδυμίωνα
stance_events:

limits: This is an illustrative analogy supporting the cyclical-generation thesis. It does not assert a doctrine about sleep or Endymion; it is a thought-experiment showing the absurd result if reciprocal generation between opposites is denied.
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0072
source_work: phaedo
stephanus_span: 72b-72c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 72b-72c
  start_marker: 72b
  end_marker: 72c

  end_char: 36632
  text_sha256: 7055f83cce036c2aa56311dd23dfa51496e2ec692f639f7e3ba00447538bebff
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If everything that partakes of life were to die and after dying remain dead in that form and not come back to life, then necessarily all things would end up dead and nothing would live.
greek_terms:
  - ἀποθνῄσκοι
  - ἀναβιώσκοιτο
  - πολλὴ ἀνάγκη

limits: This record registers the life-death application of the cyclical-generation thesis. It does not assert that resurrection actually occurs; Socrates presents it as a necessary consequence of denying reciprocal generation between opposites, addressed to Cebes as part of the argument for the soul's continued existence.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0074
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 72d-72e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If living things come to be only from the living, but living things die, everything would be consumed into the state of being dead — so there must be a process of coming back to life from the dead for the cycle to continue.
greek_terms:
  - ἀναβιώσκεσθαι
  - ἐκ τῶν τεθνεώτων τοὺς ζῶντας γίγνεσθαι
stance_events:

The claim is part of a larger argument chain and this segment does not contain its full defense.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0075

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.

```




```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0092

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.

```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0095
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 75a-75b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 75a-75b
  start_marker: 75a
  end_marker: 75b

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The thought of the Equal itself cannot arise from anything other than seeing, touching, or some other sense-perception; all these sources are the same for this purpose.

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0097
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 75a-75b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 75a-75b
  start_marker: 75a
  end_marker: 75b
  start_char: 42471
  end_char: 43479

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Before we began to see, hear, and otherwise perceive, we must have already acquired knowledge of the Equal itself, that it is, if we were going to refer the equal things from sense-perceptions to it — recognizing that all such sensibles yearn to be like it but are inferior to it.

  - ἐπιστήμην
  - αὐτοῦ τοῦ ἴσου

limits: This is the conclusion of the argument from the previous premises; the record does not claim that this thesis is Socrates' own view rather than a step in a jointly accepted dialectical argument, nor does it address whether the knowledge is latent or occurrent.
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0099

stephanus_span: 75c-75d

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Before birth we must have acquired knowledge of the equal itself, the greater, the smaller, and all such things — not only the equal but also the beautiful itself, the good itself, the just, the holy, and everything upon which we set the seal "what it is."

  - αὐτοῦ τοῦ καλοῦ
  - δικαίου

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0100

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0359

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0109
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 76d-76e

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If the recurring entities (the beautiful, the good, and all such being) exist and we refer sense-data back to them, discovering them as a pre-existing possession of ours, then it is necessary that our soul exists even before we are born.

  - καλόν τέ τι καὶ ἀγαθὸν
  - οὐσία
  - ψυχὴν

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0110
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0117

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.

  - αὐτὸ καθ’ αὑτό

review_status: accepted
```





```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0123
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 79c

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: When the soul makes use of the body to investigate something through sight, hearing, or any other sense — that is, when it investigates something through the body via sense-perception — then the soul is dragged by the body into things that are never in the same state, and the soul itself wanders, is troubled, and reels as if drunk, because it is grasping such things.
greek_terms:
  - ἕλκεται
  - πλανᾶται
  - ταράττεται
  - εἰλιγγιᾷ
  - μεθύουσα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 79c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0377
limits: The claim describes a causal-psychological mechanism but is stated as recalling a previous point. The description is metaphorical and does not by itself prove the soul's separability.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0124
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 79d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 79d

  end_marker: 79d

  text_sha256: 7541e0403ddfc53310f8303d4ac3b43b87bc694e9139bc2a6be55b7df7946f07
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: When the soul investigates by itself alone, it goes to the pure, always-existing, immortal, and unchanging realm, and being akin to it, the soul remains with it whenever it is by itself and is permitted to do so, ceases from wandering, and stays constant in relation to those unchanging things; and this experience of the soul is called wisdom (phronesis).
greek_terms:
  - καθαρόν
  - ἀθάνατον
  - φρόνησις
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 79d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 79d
      start_marker: 79d
      end_marker: 79d

      end_char: 54350

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0378
limits: The record does not establish that the soul can in fact exist by itself apart from the body; it describes what would happen if it could. The identification of phronesis as the soul's condition when in contact with the unchanging realm is asserted but not independently argued here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```

```yaml

```




```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0141
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 85c-85d

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: To achieve certainty about the soul in this life is either impossible or exceedingly difficult, but to fail to test the arguments in every way and to give up before exhausting the inquiry would be the mark of a very weak man.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 85c-85d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []

review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0142
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 85c-85d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 85c-85d
  start_marker: 85c
  end_marker: 85d
  start_char: 67337
  end_char: 68342
  text_sha256: 61b00552769e8329ad2effc7325a7bb124cb4655264959344dc05b8d08f7f2d7
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: When certainty is impossible, one should take the best and most irrefutable human account and, riding it as on a raft, risk the voyage through life, unless one can travel more safely on a firmer vehicle—some divine account.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 85c-85d
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: This record does not establish that Simmias is claiming a divine account is available; he presents it as a superior alternative that may be unavailable. The record does not establish that Socrates endorses the raft principle.
review_status: rejected
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0144
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 85e-86c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 85e-86c
  start_marker: 85e
  end_marker: 86c
  start_char: 68342
  end_char: 70011
  text_sha256: a1715a49fdcbfc8593edb0799f3ea85f315c5afa92e447ead1845c85f854bcbb
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If the soul is an attunement, then when the body is slackened or stretched beyond measure by illnesses and other evils, the soul must perish immediately, even though it is most divine, while the bodily remains persist for a long time—just as with other attunements in sounds and in the works of craftsmen.
greek_terms:
  - ἁρμονία
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 86c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  []
limits: This record does not establish that Simmias affirms the attunement thesis; he poses it as a conditional objection to Socrates' earlier arguments. The record does not establish that the conditional consequence (perishability) is claimed as a fact about the soul.
review_status: rejected
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0148

stephanus_span: 89d-89e

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Misology and misanthropy arise from the same cause: trusting someone or some argument completely without craft, and then repeatedly discovering that the trusted person or argument is worthless."

  - μισανθρωπία
  - ἄνευ τέχνης

observation_ids:

review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0150
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 90a

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - πλείστους
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0153
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 90b-90c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Those who spend their time on antilogikoi logoi end up thinking that nothing in pragmata or logoi is sound or stable, but that all beings are turned upside down like things in the Euripus and remain nowhere in anything.
greek_terms:
  - ἀντιλογικοὺς λόγους

    stephanus_span: 90b-90c

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0154
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 90b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 90b
  start_marker: 90b
  end_marker: 90b

  end_char: 78933
  text_sha256: bc7459a0652a1939316656c7b1cc6f68159cf26461cfe13dfb908ceed792b2ae
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: The cause of misology is not exactly the same as that of misanthropy with respect to human beings, but is similar in that a person trusts a logos to be true without the craft concerning logoi, and then later judges it false, sometimes being so and sometimes not.
greek_terms:
  - ἄνευ τῆς περὶ τοὺς λόγους τέχνης
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 90b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 90b
      start_marker: 90b
      end_marker: 90b
      start_char: 78467
      end_char: 78933
      text_sha256: bc7459a0652a1939316656c7b1cc6f68159cf26461cfe13dfb908ceed792b2ae
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0401
limits: This distinguishes the logos case from the human-being case while identifying the common lack of craft; it does not prescribe a positive method for avoiding the error.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0155
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 90c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 90c

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It would be a pitiable experience if, there being in fact some true and stable logos that can be grasped, someone should end up hating logoi because of encountering logoi that sometimes seem true and sometimes not, and blame not oneself and one's own lack of craft but instead shift the blame onto the logoi themselves.
greek_terms:
  - οἰκτρὸν
  - ἀληθοῦς
  - βεβαίου
  - λόγου
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 90c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
      stephanus_span: 90c
      start_marker: 90c
      end_marker: 90c

      end_char: 79409

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0402
limits: The thesis is a conditional warning about the pitiable nature of misology given that a true logos exists; the record does not assert that such a true and stable logos has been demonstrated in the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0156

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: One should blame one's own lack of skill rather than the arguments when arguments appear alternately true and false, lest one come to hate and revile all arguments and be deprived of the truth and knowledge of things that are.

  []

  - kind: reported

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```


```yaml

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: Socrates instructs Simmias and Cebes to care little for Socrates and much more for the truth, agreeing if he seems to speak truly but resisting with every argument if he does not, so that he does not deceive both himself and them through eagerness and depart like a bee leaving its sting.
greek_terms:
  - μέλιττα
  - κέντρον
stance_events:
  - kind: reported

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0164

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates argues that the harmony thesis is inconsistent with the recollection thesis because a harmony is a composite thing that cannot exist before the elements from which it is composed, whereas the recollection thesis requires the soul to exist before entering a human form and body.

  - ἁρμονίαν
  - σύνθετον
  - συγκειμένην
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0171
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 93a-93b

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - ἁρμονία

review_status: accepted
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0175
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 93d-94a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt
  stephanus_span: 93d-94a
  start_marker: 93d
  end_marker: 94a
  start_char: 86218
  end_char: 87609
  text_sha256: 7e092d76cc2ae0e11cc0dc655254f1442afb3cb74c1ab1a602c7f2bf52c1b484
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If soul is a harmony, then all souls are equally good, since no soul is more or less a soul than another, and if virtue is harmony and vice disharmony, no soul could participate in vice.
greek_terms:
  - ἁρμονία
  - ψυχή
  - κακία
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 93d-94a
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: "This is a reductio ad absurdum: from the harmony hypothesis it follows that no soul could be bad, which contradicts the agreed fact that some souls are bad. It does not establish what the soul positively is."
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0176
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 94b-94c

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The soul rules over all things in a human being, especially the intelligent soul, and the soul opposes bodily affections — for instance, pulling against thirst toward not drinking, against hunger toward not eating, and in countless other cases.

  - ἄρχειν
  - ἐναντιουμένην
```

```yaml
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0178
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 95b-95c

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Cebes' request reduces to this: it must be demonstrated that the soul is indestructible and immortal, so that when a philosophical man expects to fare well in death his confidence is not foolish."

  - ἀνώλεθρόν
  - ἀθάνατον

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0179
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 95c-95d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: report
content: Cebes holds that showing the soul to be strong, godlike, and pre-existent before human birth does not yet demonstrate immortality; it indicates only that the soul is long-lived and pre-existed for an immense time, while entry into a human body may be the beginning of its destruction, like a disease.
greek_terms:
  - ἀθάνατον
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 95c-95d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_phaedo_0422
limits: This is Socrates' summary of Cebes' position; it does not state Socrates' own view, and the reported claim is presented as the challenge that must be answered.
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0181
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 96a-96b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/phaedo.txt

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: report
content: "Socrates, when young, was extraordinarily eager for the wisdom called inquiry into nature, supposing it a grand thing to know the causes of each thing: why each comes to be, why it perishes, and why it is."

  - περὶ φύσεως ἱστορίαν
  - αἰτίας

    stephanus_span: 96a-96b

review_status: accepted
```







```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0200
source_work: Phaedo
stephanus_span: 99d-99e
source_ref:

  text_sha256: 16ef2fd9938fbc6b8b37db1c543dea3380306389c299d75d0c0e89c0189050ab
speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: One must take refuge in logoi and examine the truth of beings in them, just as one must view an eclipse in water rather than directly, lest the soul be blinded by looking at things with the senses.

  - λόγους
  - τὴν ψυχὴν τυφλωθείην

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: This is a negative methodological rule (avoid direct sense-perception inquiry) that prepares the positive method of hypotheses; it does not itself describe the hypothesis method.
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates hypothesizes that there exists a beautiful-itself by itself, a good-itself, a large-itself, and all such things; from these he expects to demonstrate the cause and discover that the soul is deathless.

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0203
source_work: Phaedo

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If anything else is beautiful besides the beautiful-itself, it is beautiful for no other reason than because it partakes of that beautiful; and likewise for all things.

  - μετέχει
  - καλοῦ
  - αἰτίᾳ
stance_events:

    stephanus_span: 100c

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - κοινωνία
  - τῷ καλῷ

```

```yaml

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_phaedo_0233

speaker: ΦΑΙΔ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: To complete the proof that soul is not destroyed when death approaches, it must also be agreed that the deathless is indestructible; otherwise the three and the odd could perish and be replaced by the even.
greek_terms:
  - ἀθάνατον
  - ἀνώλεθρόν
  - ψυχῇ
  - ἀπόλλυσθαι
  - περιττὸν
  - ἄρτιον

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: "Marks a gap in the proof: not admitting a contrary is not yet indestructibility. The odd is non-even but not indestructible, so the soul's deathlessness must also entail indestructibility for the proof to hold."
review_status: accepted
```



